The Landtag of Lower Austria is based on a decision of the Land Government of the Netherlands. On 28 May 2001 the Commission adopted a proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to turnover taxes. This decision was taken in the Landtag – with the consent of the SPÖ – but that does not mean the form in which these funds are to be taken. [[Abg. Donabauer: With your consent!]] [[Demonstratetive applause with the ÖVP]] [[Abg. May. Tancsits: Bank Burgenland!]] It is, of course, possible to make use of housing loans, and there is nothing objectionable about making such a decision. But it is most objectionable to proceed as was the case in Lower Austria. In fact, the person to whom the Pouvoir is granted is open to two paths: the path of serious charge, the safe charge, the risk-free charge to be chosen for taxpayers, and the way to the casino. The relevant constitutional provisions – both in the Austrian Federal Constitution and in the Lower Austrian Constitution – have standardised the principle of applying economy and effectiveness in the use of resources. In other words, each person acting must comply with these constitutionally standardised principles in the use of resources, i.e., comply with economy, economy and effectiveness. This also applies to the case of private administration. Even if I invest these funds via an investment company, this principle applies. That means it is not free for the person acting to say: I am investing as I want! how this can be done by an individual, by a private person who can go to the casino with the money or to the stock market or just make low-risk claims. That means that the state is much stronger bound. In this respect, the State is constitutionally bound in a corset and has to comply with the principles of economy, economy and effectiveness. Since December 2000, any federal purpose has been guaranteed. The removal of the purpose is a purely land-based asset. Since the administration of the land assets is a matter for the private administration of the country, the Federal legislator is not responsible for lack of competence. As the responsibility for an investment is therefore the responsibility of the country, political control is also the responsibility of the respective country day or financial control to the Court of Auditors or the national accounting court. Even if the Federation could restrict the countries here, it would not advise to do so. All political parties, at least in principle, are committed to federalism and to the efforts to create as closed areas of competence as possible in the countries and to avoid double-tracking in the local authorities. It would be absurd if, in view of the objective without need, the Federation would interfere in the management of national assets. By the way: At the legal level, what rules should the federal government make for the use of state resources? Detailed rules on interest rates, debt securities or the like are likely to be expiring from the outset. A flexible response is essential especially in the field of exposure. So there are only general statements on the best possible presumption according to the well-known principles of economy, economy and so on. That wouldn't really help anyone. Therefore, the National Council is certainly not the competent body for a discussion of rules and controls of the country's claims. Such a discussion could only take place in the Landtag. The National Council is, in any case, the wrong place because of lack of competence, and I can only explain it to myself with one election date that we are talking about this today. ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e5585	Mr President. High house! When we were on the 26th. The situation in Iraq was discussed here in the House of Lords on February, but there was still a hope for peace. The war started a week ago. For a week, bombs have been falling, people are dying. But hundreds of thousands in all parts of the earth have been demonstrating for peace for days. Not only do they ask themselves many questions, legitimate questions – and not always do the answers with these legitimate questions –: Was it really enough for peace – in the international institutions, in the state offices, in Baghdad itself? Why have such late-looking compromise or conciliation proposals been put on the table – proposals that have been discussed very long before by smaller countries such as Ireland or larger than Canada in the UN Security Council, such as very specific requirements and tests that Saddam Hussein should have fulfilled, and deadlines that would not have been in the infinite but would have resulted in a reasonable review with a 30-day deadline? Why has the comprehensive report of the weapons inspectors – 83 pages containing the open questions to Saddam Hussein and his regime – been put on the table so late? What does this failure of political mediation mean for the international community, for the United Nations? What does this mean for international law? In this context, I refer to the statements of the Social Democratic President of the Federal Foreign Committee in the German Bundestag, who has stated that the two reasons for exception from the general renunciation of violence, the disregard of war, as laid down in the UN Charter, are no longer sufficient, on the one hand, the right to self-defense and on the other hand, the use of force authorized by a UN Security Council decision, when world peace is threatened. He believes – and many international lawyers believe this with him – that a third reason has been added since the Kosovo War and since Bosnia: Violence may also be applied if a humanitarian disaster threatens, or if States no longer bear violence, but non-governmental, terrorist or criminal organisations. For many actors, this state-of-the-art right simply does not or does not fit fully, and it therefore needs to be further developed. But it also raises the question: How seriously do we Europeans themselves take the objective and the claim of a common foreign and security policy if many Member States, in the case of Iraq, immediately divide themselves into groups, without, in fact, a common basis, seeking consensus within the European family, perhaps a more laborious way, but, I believe, the only right and only goal-setting European way, ladies and gentlemen? This and nothing else we, the Austrian Federal Government and the Foreign Minister, meant when we said that we were in the middle – of course not between a dictator and the democracies, but we are in the European centre on the ground of a common position, which we want to work together to develop which serves the peace that is devoted to the primacy of politics, but at the same time also expresses the determination in our approach to terror and dictatorship. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! We must not forget that it probably has its reason that, in a country like Iraq with about 20 million inhabitants, 4 million people have already become refugees, where wars have concluded that their own population, especially the suffering Kurdish people, has already become the target of the dictator several times. And that is why this longing for peace and this longing for security, which has manifested itself in the hearts and also in public among many people across the continent, is going on! It is about the deep longing of generations to realize peace and security by means other than violence. And we Europeans have just experienced wars – perhaps not our generation, but the elderly, the generation who are still alive, who enjoy their deserved retirement today, have experienced wars. She also experienced the consequences of wars, she had to complain of victims in every family. Many other great nations, such as the Americans, never had anything comparable in their territory. At the same time, we Europeans – especially we Europeans – should be sensitive, perhaps also sensible, to deal with dictators, because we have also experienced what it can mean if a dictator does not fall into the arms of the state community in good time. I therefore believe that Europe also has the right to express itself on this issue and the right as well as the obligation and responsibility to take a common approach here. War is – and it is in the UN Charter – the last of the political means. Finally, war is the sad result of a failed policy. I mean, this war would have been prevented. He would have been, above all, to prevent Saddam Hussein. In the last twelve years, he has always had the power to comply with the 17 United Nations resolutions, namely by disarming his multiple weapons of mass destruction. This would have brought an end to the sanctions imposed on Iraq, which were primarily imposed on the people, and it would have been the war to be avoided. The way to this war, ladies and gentlemen – I want to express it very openly – is ultimately also a chain of many serious political misevaluations: Saddam Hussein obviously expected to continue his game in the play of the community against each other endlessly. The Americans underestimated the willingness of some members of the Security Council to stick to their uncompromising No to a military action. And these, in turn, underestimated the US' determination to implement the serious consequences threatened in resolution 1441 even without explicit authorization of the World Security Council. The way to this war is therefore also a history of misevaluations and a failure of politics. I believe that we must also accept this and represent ourselves to the public, but at the same time we must encourage people and make them clear that we are drawing a lesson from this bitterness that many people have taken. I would like to say another thing: The media play a role in this war as never before in history. They carry the war with all its atrocities into our living rooms. Here, too, we are called upon to ensure that the boundaries of humanity and human dignity are not exceeded. From the necessary information to operate the display, from objective reporting to manipulation, from the necessary corrective to the tool of propaganda, it is often only a small step. Ladies and gentlemen and watchers on the televisions! The National Security Council in these days – and I think this is very, very important – has established a common line of Austria on the basis of the conclusions of the European Council which took place last week. Together, we have achieved something very important: In this critical situation, Austria speaks with one voice! [[General condolences.]] That is right, that is good, and I would also like to expressly thank all the political parties represented here in the House. When it comes to such fundamental questions of world peace, international law, security, we need this red and white consensus! And that's not a washed compromise. ==References====External links== Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I believe that we are trying successfully to involve European responsibility and our own legitimate security needs. We insist that military action requires the empowerment of the World Security Council. We reaffirm that the neutral Austria will not be involved in any military operations against Iraq, nor will it grant overflight rights. We give particular priority, above all, to restoring the full authority of the United Nations. Only the UN as the only one can ultimately be able to take on full responsibility for the construction of democratic structures and the protection of ethnic and religious minorities, of course, as soon as possible after the end of the fights. We want to do everything within the framework of the European Union and the UN to guarantee at least that minorities – especially the Kurds in Northern Iraq – have struggled under great effort. It is important that we Europeans stand by this people, who have so often been disappointed by real or supposed alliance partners. [[General condolences.]] It should also be called on the Turkish Government to refrain from any military intrusion into Iraqi territory. Ladies and gentlemen! We therefore believe that dialogue with the Arab world must be promoted. It must also be countered that the world community measures with different standards as regards the need to comply with internationally significant resolutions. I think it is very essential that we do not measure by two measures. [[General condolences.]] How does the internal security of Austria continue? – The Federal Government, with the greatest possible care, takes all necessary measures to protect the citizens both domestically and in the crisis regions themselves. I say quite openly here: No government in the world can rule out acts of terrorism, but we must do the right thing to prevent terrorism. There is no reason at present to believe that Austria is subject to special additional risks. There is reason for alertness, but not too fear. What is the Federal Government doing to protect citizens? – The Ministry of Foreign Affairs is in constant contact with the representative authorities to protect Austrian citizens. Around the clock, a telephone service has been established, which has already been used by thousands of people. The Ministry of Defence monitors the state area including the airspace – especially in the current situation, a particularly important case of defence and neutrality. The Ministry of the Interior has significantly increased the protection of at least endangered persons and objects and, together with the NGOs and the countries, has taken steps to take in the reception of Iraqi refugees who may be coming to Austria. The preparation and coordination of humanitarian measures at national and international level has also begun. Another question: Did Austria do enough to avoid this conflict, to prevent conflict? – We should keep the sense of proportions here. In view of its size and geographical situation, Austria is only able to contribute effectively to conflict prevention with other European countries. Peacekeeping and peacekeeping are not alone. Therefore, we have worked very intensively – in the person of the Foreign Minister or I as head of government – for a common voice in Europe. As a UN-Sitz State, we have actively contributed to the resumption of the talks between the Iraqi leadership and the UN-Chemical Inspectors. We have made available to the UN Austrian inspectors and experts. At the diplomatic level, we have made efforts to increase the potential for peace in contacts with the Arab States. We have begun the dialogue of civilizations – Christianity, Judaism, Islam. We want to continue this precisely in these times. It is about not being torn off from this dialogue beyond the Atlantic, but also from the Arab world. There's something else I'm proud of, which we can all be proud of: That we have not only been in Austria since the 11th century. September 2001 or only since now have a working relationship between the different population groups and religions. In Austria, the peaceful living together between Christians, Jews and Muslims is daily reality. I would like to thank all those who have contributed to a concrete peace process. [[General condolences.]] This is not so obvious, by the way, because it was still in the monarchy, in 1912, when Islam was recognized as an official religion in our homeland. This was actually an example of soloism at the time and is still a model for many other European countries today. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that we are taking a very important step with this common attitude. But in reality – I say this quite openly here – of course political work in this area begins in the post-war period. We will be very active and united in this. The second theme at the European Council last week in Brussels was the so-called Lisbon Strategy. After a third of the road up to 2010 it should be asked: Where are we? Are we really on the way to making the Union the most competitive and dynamic economic area in the world – an economic area capable of achieving sustainable growth with more and better jobs and greater social cohesion? – In 2001, the ecological dimension was added to the Gothenburg Council. - We're making progress. There is a slight increase in per capita income from America, for example. There is a slight increase – by 2 percent from 62 to 64 percent – in the employment rate. However, we must say that in some areas progress is simply not the same as we wanted it to be and how it would be achieved with a common European line. Austria is in employment with a total rate of 68.4 percent and a female employment rate of 60.1 percent is close or already in line with the targets. The employment rate for older workers is substantially too low. Therefore, there are also very specific measures in the government programme: the reform of the AMS, shorter mediation periods, the expansion of the early warning system, the gradual expiry of the early retirement pension and concrete assistance to keep older workers in employment. What is important is the following: Together with Finland and Portugal, Austria has made an excuse for a employment task force at European level. That has also been decided. This working group, which will present its report in one year, will be headed by the former Dutch head of government Wim Kok. Thus, an Austrian claim has been implemented. Our social partnership experience will and should be involved in this process. The second important topic was education, research and innovation. Europe is falling back, but Austria is catching up. Ten years ago we had a research rate of not even 1.5 percent, five years ago one of 1.8 percent. We have now reached a rate of 2%. We have spent €4 billion over the last three years. This is by 20 percent or 600 million € more than in the years before. In this way, ladies and gentlemen, we want and must move forward in order to be able to make the most of Austria. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Austria is also outstanding on the essential issue of social cohesion. There we have a top place, are already in the top 3, are in third place. In addition to the very favourable situation in the labour market in Europe, we have the third lowest unemployment rate – above all our school system, which is responsible for this success. In the category of school dropout Austria has one of the lowest rates, namely second place. We are continuing to work on improving significantly in these areas. It is also in the environment. In terms of the environment, we are taking a leading role throughout the European Union, not just since today. But in this top position – we are number 1 in Europe – Austria can rightly be proud. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I do not want to paint too rusty a picture here, because we have many structural reforms still pending, no question – and we will therefore continue on this courageous path of liberalisation in economic law. And of course we will continue our priorities in the areas of education, research and employment of older workers. I would like to say the following: Austria has a first-class starting point to really advance among the best three European countries in the coming years, by 2010. That must be our ambition, and we can also achieve that, ladies and gentlemen, I am 100% convinced! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] To give you a little courage here: Our country has improved very well in some of the most important indicators, such as private entrepreneurs' investment – and this is one of the most important comparisons at all. In this respect, Austria now has the second best place throughout the European Union. As far as per capita income is concerned, Austria has progressed from the fifth to the fourth place; in terms of labour productivity, it has improved by one place; in the case of growth, there is a four-place leap forward; in real unit labour costs, we are in second place in the European Union. [[Abg. May. Worm: Women don't happen?- Oh, my God!]] And what the budget balance is about, we have made up for four places – not quite perfect, but after all. According to the low-income countries in the Mediterranean, which usually have a particularly low inflation rate, Austria is the most price-stable country. As a result of Telekom's liverization, we made a clear leap forward in the long-distance talks, from the previous place to the sixth place within a year. Further improvements will, of course, be reflected in the rankings. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Oils.]] Therefore, ladies and gentlemen: I believe that we are on course with this interim report, although not yet where we want to see ourselves and where our real potential lies, but I believe that if we move forward on this courageous reform course in Austria, we will soon have achieved the goal of being among the best three countries in Europe.

However, the ban on violence in the United Nations Statute is the basis of international law at all if I have understood it correctly. And there are very few exceptions to this prohibition of violence – my previous speakers have already outlined it – especially the right to self-defense or intervention, including military intervention, genocide or similar gross violations of human rights. An action with a mandate can also be wrong. I think it is right that Austria has been fully involved in this group, ladies and gentlemen! When the first war disputes began, we have tightened the security preparations accordingly and taken the following six immediate measures: First of all, additional measures have been taken to protect persons and property, in particular the protection of those bodies and representative authorities involved in this conflict, as well as of economic branches from exposed nations and security officials located in Austria. We are in a very good dialogue with them. Secondly, we secured the airport and all international connections in Austria. Security level 2 was activated again. Thirdly, there is close coordination within the Federal Government. On Friday at 8 a.m., the Federal Chancellor asked for the security coordination. Refugees can come from the region who have suffered under the regime, and refugees can come who have a completely different status due to the present threat. That is why this measure makes sense, and I am a little proud that we were the first country in Europe to ensure exactly this complete, comprehensive protection for refugees. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We are going our way for internal security very consistently. Thus, under the Federal Constitutional Act, the federal budget was or is to be held as a budget provisorium under Article 51(2), with a legal limit. If no legislative commission were to be adopted, the corresponding impact would have on the financial markets that would naturally respond to it. This limit is 50 percent of the maximum amounts of the financial debt of the previous year, i.e. 2002. Once these commitments have been entered into, this limit is reached. ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e9646	From the government bank, Mr. Federal Minister for Finance has taken power. Grasser to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e9667	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. High house! Allow me to use the remaining 4 minutes of speaking time until we interrupt at 3 p.m. to not reflect the broad debate we had – from the Cassascrum to the foundations of financial policy – but to refer specifically to the legislative budget and to allow me to become a little more basic and detailed at the end of the billing. [[Abg. Oillinger: "Grasser crash"? What's that?]] Ladies and gentlemen! Article 51 of the Federal Constitutional Act distinguishes between the automatic and the statutory budget. We all know the rule case as we introduce budgets, federal financial laws, budget accompanying laws, usually ten weeks before the beginning of the new year, usually there is a decision-making in December. But, of course, the fathers of our Constitution have also taken precautions in case this is not possible. We are now exactly in this situation. An automatic budget provisional is in force. This is not new, that is the case in Austria for the third time, so it is not an upsetting or particularly problematic situation. Finally, we had this situation in 1999 when Rudolf Edlinger could not bring the budget to the National Council in autumn and therefore there was no decision. This Article 51 of the Federal Constitutional Act provides in paragraph 5, that on the basis of an automatic budget procedure, financial debt may be entered only up to half of the maximum amounts provided for in the last Federal Finance Act. These ceilings amounted to about €22 billion. 50 percent of which we are allowed to accept on the basis of the automatic provisorium, i.e. about € 11 billion. In agreement with the Court of Auditors, we know that we will exceed this limit of €11 billion in about the course of April. After that, we could only meet our commitments with short-term funds – so-called cash-strengtheners. This would be much more expensive for taxpayers, it would result in additional taxes of beyond € 15 million. Clearly, such a course makes no sense. The most reasonable way is: If we ensure the liquidity of the Republic on a legal basis, we ensure it on the basis of a legal commission, we save the taxpayer at least € 15 million, we send a clear signal to the financial markets, a signal of security, a signal of stability! When we look at ourselves: What problem do we have?, then we note that there is a very clear, best solution. This best solution has been confirmed by the President of the Court of Auditors, Professor DDr. Fresh as well as by Dr. Eder from the Federal Finance Agency, which is the decision of the legal budget commission. I also think that this signal of stability, this signal of security – ensuring the liquidity of the Republic, ensuring the ability to pay, especially in more difficult and more unstable times, is a very important decision for taxpayers. That is why I would ask you to put this signal together today and, if possible, to carry this legal budget procedure together. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e13188	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! The Schüssel I government took place three years ago to bring about a change in budget policy. A turning point in budgetary policy means introducing reforms. Today, the 2001 financial statements are on the agenda, and we can see that the reforms introduced have taken place in fiscal consolidation. Dear ladies and gentlemen! For the first time since 1974, i.e. nearly 30 years ago, it has succeeded in making no new debts in 2001. The public budgets of the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities generated a budget surplus of 0.3 percent of GDP. [[Abg. Gradwell: And how much of it did the Federation achieve?]] We should therefore thank the countries and communities for their contribution to the consolidation of the budget today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The 2001 budget estimate was drawn up at a time when economic researchers forecast a growth in gross domestic product of 4.2 percent. We now know that economic growth has reached around 2.6 percent of GDP. It is therefore all the more welcome that, despite the flattening of economic growth, a budget surplus has been achieved. In the meantime, the corresponding budget figures for 2002 have been announced by the Ministry of Finance: There is a minus of 0.6 percent. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Despite the weak and flattering economic situation, this is an excellent result! For comparison only: As mentioned today, Germany has a budget deficit of 3.7 percent, France of 3.1 percent. The EU average is minus 1.9 percent. Dear ladies and gentlemen! As Mr Matznetter mentioned that there had not been a proper budget consolidation, then a few figures: In 2001, consolidation spread to around 50.7 percent on the expenditure side and 49.3 percent on the revenue side – i.e. fifty-fifty. In the last year of 2002, on the other hand, 64 percent of the need for consolidation was already covered on the part of expenditure and only 36 percent on the revenue side. Dear ladies and gentlemen! That is, we can talk about a successful structural measure, the consolidation course has been continued on a sustainable basis! This is also the prerequisite for the successful implementation of the government programme of the Schussel II cabinet. However, it is also a prerequisite for the largest tax reform of the Second Republic, which is now being prepared and is also to be implemented. In this legislative period, €3 billion will be spent in two stages. This reform package will also achieve a reduction in the tax rate to 42.9% by the end of the current legislative period. The government's objective of achieving a balanced budget across the entire economic cycle – that is to buy a deficit if growth is low, while achieving surpluses in medium and strong growth – is a new quality in budget policy! Finally, I should like to say that my group will, of course, be happy to give its assent to this financial statements in 2001.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e13519	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Grasser reported. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e13541	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. High house! Austria has made new debts for 30 years each year. Regardless of whether there has been an economic weakness or a high level of economic activity – that is, the economy has run, high employment rate, high corporate profits – in Austria, new debts have been made every year in these last nearly 30 years. This led to our having to take over a financial debt of about €121 billion in 2000, which brings interest payments to an order of €7.3 billion. Every day we pay €20 million in interest. And if I say we pay it, then of course it's the taxpayer. We all pay over higher taxes and charges these interest rates for these very high financial debts that have been incurred there. That is why it was necessary and right for us to say in the Federal Government during the last legislative period, in the Cabinet Schüssel I: We need a stability-oriented fiscal policy, we need a financial policy capable of complying with international commitments such as the Stability and Growth Pact. We need a financial policy that does not irresponsibly shift to the future, to the next generations, but allows fair and fair maintenance of a generation agreement, and a financial policy that, on the one hand, creates funds in the long term and allows investment in research and development, in education, in infrastructure and thus in growth policy, and on the other hand, is the prerequisite for sustainable discharge, which we have also committed ourselves. Therefore, a proper financial policy, important for Austria – a turning point in the financial policy we have sought. Ladies and gentlemen! If you look at the 2001 federal accounts, you have to say that for the first time there has been a federal government that has given itself a transparent goal, a clearly verifiable and measurable goal. We said in 2000 that we want to achieve a balanced budget over the economic cycle, and we want to achieve a zero deficit in Austria for the first time in 2002 for more than 30 years. We have already achieved this zero deficit in 2001, i.e. a year earlier than planned. Today it has fallen several times the sentence that I have been allowed to bring twice in a budget speech, namely: “A good day begins with a renovated budget.” I am convinced that the year 2001 is symbolic of a reformed budget, is proof that the change in financial policy has succeeded and that this turn has been possible for the taxpayer, for the Republic of Austria. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen of the opposition! If you're honest, that's a bit of a question to you – you're always honest, I've assumed it – then you probably have to admit that you were surprised first that the Federal Government had the courage to say we're setting ourselves such an ambitious, ambitious goal and we're officially saying: [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig: We are always honest!]] [[Abg. Gradwelt: Are we always!]] The target is the zero deficit in 2002. I think that surprised you back then. Secondly, I think you have to admit, no one of you has agreed that we will actually achieve this goal. 2001 shows black on white, we have achieved this goal. [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig: At what price!]] I would like to thank all those who have come to this state and contributed to this achievement, as I have done before other speakers. That means: Thanks to the taxpayers, thanks to the workers, thanks to the economy, because they have carried this financial policy with home mind to a very high degree from the beginning, thanks also to those who have helped us in the expenditure reforms. Ladies and gentlemen! We have more than 70 administrative reform projects running at federal level alone. It is not natural for public officials to take part in this and have tried to reduce public expenditure accordingly. Of course, I would also like to thank the countries, cities and communities, because their surpluses have also made a great contribution to the fact that we were able to achieve this surplus of 0.3% of GDP in general. Of course I would also like to thank you, ladies and gentlemen, who have enabled us at the legal level to implement this budgetary policy with the various measures so that we can achieve these objectives. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, some criticisms have also come, and of course you can ask: How did you get this, how was it on the revenue side, which one-time effects were included and how does this look in detail? – You can do that, too, that is justified. Nevertheless, I have hoped, I say – perhaps a bit illusionary – that the opposition has also given an acceptance, a recognition for the broad line, because I have also heard from you that one has once said: Add the zero deficit to the Constitution! Once again, one has advertised in the election campaign and said: Social democracy also stands for a policy to pursue the balanced budget over the economic cycle. We didn't want to use this recognition for Alfred Finz and for my person. I just think it is important for the country because this financial policy, this economic policy is the right way for Austria – a way for more growth, more jobs and more prosperity in our country. That is why I believe that we should have this size of party policy, including crossing borders, and say: This is the right way for Austria. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Ladies and gentlemen! We started at the beginning of the year 2000: Austria was the number 15 out of 15 Member States of the European Union in financial policy. We were finally in the European Union as far as the deficit is concerned. [[Abg. Dr. Niederweiser: Bullshit!]] We were already number 7 in the European Union in 2001. Within two years, we have pushed forward eight places and achieved this surplus of 0.3% of gross domestic product, while the average of the European Union, the other 14 Member States, is 0.9 percent. This means: 0.9 percent deficit to 0.3 percent surplus in Austria. Other countries: Germany 2.8% deficit, Portugal 4.2% deficit, France 1.4% deficit. That is to say, we have jointly managed to reintegrate Austria into the ranks of the fiscal stable states, and this has been an extremely important success that we have achieved – a success that – whether it is the OECD, whether that is the International Monetary Fund, or whether it is the Commission of the European Union – has also been recognized. [[Complementary with the EIA and the Free Trade Unions]] It has often been found that Austria has achieved a dramatic improvement in its public finances. In other words, we have not been waiting for it, we have taken action, we have acted and we have put forward a wealth of measures so that we can actually achieve these ambitious objectives. Ladies and gentlemen! The expenditure page has often been criticized. Example in personnel policy: We have reduced 11 430 sites in the last three years, i.e. in the last legislative period – 11,430! There have been no more Pragmatizations in a number of ministries. We have decided to take administrative reform measures to save €1.6 billion on the expenditure side. We have reformed the central offices across all departments, saved 13 sections, saved 54 groups, saved 121 departments, saved 225 units. That is, you see, we're saving in public administration, we're trying to do everything we can to open up a room for discharge. We have set up important structural reforms for Austria. Federal Minister Gehrer has implemented the university reform. We started the pension reform as early as 2000. We have been able to set an important point for employees with employee care. We have implemented a new pension scheme as a third pillar. With the childcare allowance, we have an extremely important signal for the families, for whom children are set and are therefore European champions in family support. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Of course, we have also recognized in good time that we must try to use the small national scope that we naturally have in Austria in order to try to be better at it than other countries, and have invested in research and development. It has been said that we have saved research and development. – Ladies and gentlemen! Never has more money been spent on research and development, education and infrastructure investment than in the last legislative period, because we knew that this is important for growth and employment in Austria! In December 2001, when we saw that there was a flattening of the economy, the first economic package decided to introduce a research exemption of 10 per cent on a broad basis, increased the education allowance from 9 to 20 per cent and introduced an early depreciation of 7 per cent in addition to the existing 3 per cent, i.e. 10 per cent in total, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises. We have extended tax advantages, which exist for new start-ups, also to transfers of operations and have simply renovated, ladies and gentlemen, we have reformed. We have created a good basis for Austria. Only the key values 2001 can be considered: In 2001, we had an increase of 0.7 percent, which is more than the European cut, and what is significantly more than German growth, for example. In 2001, we had an employment rate of 3,078,000 people, which was the highest level in the history of the Second Republic. [[Abg. Dr. Niederweiser: Record unemployment!]] They may not appreciate it, we are pleased to see the highest level of employment in the history of the Second Republic, which occurred in 2001. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] And we are pleased, at the same time, to tell you about the lowest unemployment rate with 138 000 unemployed – 3.6 percent, the third lowest in the European Union that we were able to achieve in 2001. The company's start-ups increased from 23 000 to 26 000 in 2001. – That means what I want to say is: Because we have reformed because we have implemented the reforms in Austria, today we are much better than Germany and much better than France. See the figures published today by the Economic Research Institute for 2002: Austria has a growth of 1 percent of the gross domestic product, Germany has a growth of 0.2 percent, the Netherlands has a growth of 0.2 percent, the average of the European Union is 0.8 percent. – Austria, because we have made a smart, growth-oriented policy, has grown more than the average of the European Union. We stand there better than all these countries, that's a great achievement! I think we should be working for workers, we should be in business, we should congratulate entrepreneurs that this was possible in a small country. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Let me conclude: We were very critical of the revenue-side versus expenditure-side reorganisation of the State budget. I've looked at this once again: If one considers the year 2001 isolated in which we had preferential effects on the revenue side, then one naturally comes to different values, but I think it is important and representative if we look at the years 2000 to 2002. Let us look at this development! The deficit – also addressed by Günter Stummvoll – which we took over was 2.3 percent of the gross domestic product – was 2.3 percent deficit at a tax rate of 44.4 percent! We achieved a deficit of 0.6 percent in 2002 at a tax rate of 44.6 percent. That is, we have reduced the deficit from 2.3 percent to 0.6 percent, i.e. by 1.7 percent of the gross domestic product, while at the same time increasing the tax rate by only 0.2 percentage points. That means, my Ladies and gentlemen, 1.5 percent of total improvement of 1.7 percent has been achieved on the expenditure side. I think this is a huge success for the Federal Government that we have succeeded! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is still to be seen that there was a growth in 1999, which is more than 2%, and that we had a growth of one percent in 2002 just now! That is, in the event of weaker growth, huge successes on the expenditure side and, I believe, significant progress over a three-year period in the consolidation of our government budget. This is the point I would like to conclude with. You can be sure that we will do everything we can to continue this consolidation, because the consolidation of state finances is also the way and the requirement for us to succeed in a sustainable discharge. The discharge has been raised, there has been a request for the vote. – Ladies and gentlemen! You will see how seriously you can take us if you look at the budget accompanying laws that go into review on Monday. There will be the first stage of the 2004 tax reform, where the tax exemption of an annual basis of €14 500 gross will be included. This means that 180 000 to 200 000 Austrians are fully excluded from the tax payment. More than 2 million Austrians will benefit! We will favour the profits not collected for small and medium-sized enterprises according to tax – non-reduced profits half-rate procedures, at least 20 percent tax payment – we will be the 13. Finally, we will abolish prepayment of VAT, and we will reduce additional labour costs, especially for older workers, because it is important for us to be able to set employment impulses here accompanied by pension reform. In conclusion: We can be happy about the best federal accounts for almost 30 years. We will continue to pursue three main objectives with all our strength: firstly, a balanced budget over the economic cycle; secondly, a large, sustainable discharge with the objective of a net relief of €3 billion in this legislative period; and thirdly, the implementation of a growth policy for Austria. Overall, then, this is a very good policy for Austria, which, as I hope, will find support across the party borders. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e13866	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen on the government! High house! In 2000, the Federal Government set itself the goal of reducing the Maastricht deficit to zero. At the time, it was under the impression of a very bad Maastricht result from 1999, which was minus 2.3 percent. [[Abg. Dr. stupid: Listen! Listen!]] At the time, Austria was the final light among all the countries of the European Union with its Maastricht deficit. [[Abg. Neudeck: Now we have it officially! Who ruled then?]] In fact, the deficit could not only be reduced in 2001 but also achieved a surplus of 0.3 percent. The result – and this clearly is also reflected in the federal accounts – was therefore positive. It has already been said by several previous speakers that for almost 30 years for the first time, there has been no deficit – calculated after Maastricht – and even a slight surplus could be achieved, a year earlier than originally planned by the government. [[Abg. Gradwelt: Thanks to the countries and communities!]] - I'll talk to you soon. The federal accounts are in itself not an audit result, which goes into detail, but rather a classic target-is comparison between the government's budgetary policies and the budget implementation. Such comparison shall be made by the Court of Auditors taking into account the naked figures. Could the government's budgetary proposals be achieved or not? . From this point of view, the Court of Auditors also made this very clear in its federal accounts and welcomed the fact that the Maastricht deficit could not only be put on zero in 2001, but that even a slight surplus was achieved. It also noted that this effort was made by all the local authorities concerned, that is, at federal level, at national and municipal level. In addition – and this also applies, and it has already been carried out – the surpluses in the Länder and the local authorities have been higher than they could have been achieved in the federal area. That too has been held. [[Call the SPÖ:Hear! Listen!]] The Court of Auditors therefore fully appreciates the efforts made at these levels of the local authorities. Of course, the Court of Auditors is not only satisfied with these naked figures, but, if you want to do so, it also has to take a look behind the scenes and ask itself: How did the very positive results of 2001 come about? . It is very soon apparent, on the basis of the material for each person who looks at the accounts, that these positive results have been achieved primarily on the revenue side. It was the strong increases on the revenue side, it was the strong increases in the tax revenue that contributed significantly to this favourable result. – I can only recall, for example, without going into more detail now – we have also expressed this in the federal accounts – that the tax revenue increased by around 80 billion schilling in 2001 compared to 2000. In the first place, this is due to the fact that certain one-off effects and preferential effects have been reported, which have already ceased in the next year, in 2002, and have not been able to change at all. Clearly, these strong increases in tax revenue also resulted in an increase in the overall fiscal burden, which rose by over 45 percent. However, I would like to add that, because there were so many preferential effects and one-off effects in 2001, then in the Federal Accounts for 2002 – we will present the Federal Accounts for 2002 in September 2003 – we will also be very clear from us that the tax rate has fallen again in 2002. However, this does not change the fact that the achievement of the zero deficit in 2001 was largely revenue-sided and less expenditure-sided, and it is important to note that the government's announcements on how to achieve the zero deficit were precisely reversed: It was referred to and it was proposed that expenditure-side measures should mainly contribute to achieving the zero deficit. [[President Dr. Fischer shall take over the chairmanship.]] High house! The Court of Auditors sees itself not only in its audit results, which he then submits to the National Council in his activity and performance reports, but also in connection with the preparation of the federal accounts as a Mahner – sometimes also as a very unpleasant Mahner; I am well aware of that – but he would not be in place in his function, if it were to be criticised in certain areas and at the same time to make recommendations about how to proceed in the future. We have already pointed out several times in the past, and so also in this federal accounts statement, that it is not intended to comply with deep structural measures to help achieve sustainable budget consolidation, i.e. not just a one-off point landing, from which one has to remove itself back to the negative, but a sustainable budget consolidation. This is where the Court of Auditors sees itself as having a meaning with the rules which come from Brussels. Again and again it is stressed that it is about the sustainability of the budget renewal and that it is not enough to achieve a goal once and then to get rid of it again. On the other hand, I would also like to point out that, in the period from 2000 to 2003, and in particular in 2001, this was the year on which the federal accounts were drawn up, measures have also been taken which have been on the expenditure side and which, I am convinced, will continue to work on the expenditure side in the future. However, I have already said several times that these measures are not sufficient for themselves, or that they could not yet be sufficient in 2001. It is therefore a recommendation from the Court of Auditors and also a reminder from the Court of Auditors – as you wish – to the Federal Government or to all other political leaders in that Republic, with the continuation of the measures taken, and to take them even more seriously than they did in the past. High house! The Court of Auditors shall respect the interests of taxpayers; it shall consider itself to be the lawyer of taxpayers. In this regard, I may recall, as taxpayers' attorneys, that in the past 10 years, the taxpayers have already been victims several times. It was this in connection with the savings packages of the years 1995 and 1996, and it was in the expired legislative period. The taxpayer has taken on these victims, but it is also expected that these victims will not only produce positive one-off effects, but that they will continue to produce positive effects. Therefore, the victims who have been paid by the taxpayer in the past should not be in vain, but should work for the future. I should therefore like to appeal again from this point of view: Let us all put together deeper structural measures to consolidate the budget from the expenditure side. I am quite optimistic on this point, because the discussion over the past few weeks and months has shown that one is well aware of the fact that deep action has to be taken, that one is aware of the fact that even more is to be done. This agreement on the need to take action, the Court of Auditors agrees, is optimistic. I should therefore like to appeal once again. You are right: Let's continue the path! Let's rehabilitate the budget together! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e17813	Mr President. High house! Before us lies the decision taken unanimously by the Constitutional Committee, the Assembly of the National Council, extending the deadline for the application for the Austrian Reconciliation Fund until 31. The Fund's duration until 31 December 2003 and the period of operation of the Fund. Recommendation for December 2004, and we are likely to follow this unanimous decision of the Constitutional Committee here too. It was necessary to extend these deadlines because it was shown that despite the enormous commitment of the staff of the Fund, the time limit originally set was too short for the management of the complex tasks associated with the reconciliation fund to be achieved on the basis of the practical experience. On this occasion, on behalf of my group, let me thank the chairman of the Committee, who is here today – Ambassador Dr. Ludwig Steiner – very, very much for his dedicated work. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Thank you, Mr Richard Wotava, too. You too have made a special effort and shown special commitment. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would like to thank, on behalf of my group, also the head of the office Dr Wolfgang Renezeder, representing the entire team, which worked very efficiently and very efficiently. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is necessary to give the partner organisations in the East a little more time to examine the applications in detail so that all the claimants can also come to their right, because it will take some time, and we do not want to close the Fund until all the cases have been processed by us. Of course, we can ask ourselves whether the extension by one year is sufficient or whether it would not be better to provide for an even longer period. The high age of the claimants, however, makes it our duty to do everything we can to do as soon as possible. within a relatively short period of time to carry out the tasks of the reconciliation fund efficiently. The extension by one year should also encourage partner organisations to work quickly and to act effectively. So far, over 97 000 applications by former slave and forced labourers have been positively completed by the Fund, in particular the efficient and unbureaucratic approach to the treatment of applications by the employees of the Fund. I would like to point out here that, as an Austrian fund, we have a very good basis and an excellent dialogue with the partner organisations in the East. It is also, of course, the fund's determination to identify all potential applicants worldwide. To this end, there has been an intensive publicity campaign in all the renowned newspapers in the world, which we can expect to achieve in this respect. However, there was also an action, namely the review of lists received from other partner organisations. For example, we have had to learn that applications have been submitted to us by the International Organization for Migration, a partner organisation of the German Foundation. We do not want to close the Fund until we have also completed these applications. It is also commendable that the Fund has also written down all persons who have ever contacted the National Fund. That is why we believe that this has also led to a widespread dispersal of the information. With the extension of the application period and the extension of the duration of the operation, all former forced workers or slave workers will be able to be recorded as far as possible – currently about 70 percent are covered – so that they will receive the voluntary symbolic performance for their injustice suffered. They always signal us that this represents a special gesture for them, because they sometimes came home to their homelands as helpers of the enemies, were suspected and persecuted, and were therefore imprisoned as a return homeer. They tell us that we are the first to consider them to be the victims of National Socialism, and this social gesture sometimes helps them almost more than the money we transfer. I am pleased that we will probably make a unanimous decision here in Parliament on this issue, and I am pleased that we can show the world with this law, with the extension of the deadline for the Fund, with the excellent work of those who deal with things in the Fund, that we are earnestly trying to work out our past.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e17917	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! My previous speaker has actually said everything to be said about the novel on the extension of the Reconciliation Fund Act or the National Fund Act. I would therefore not like to repeat this. It is important for me to say that this can lead to an appropriate end to a work that began in the 1990s under the title “Leaderness Management”. I recall the Vranitzky speech in 1991 with the admission that Austria was not only victims, but above all perpetrators, and that there is a joint responsibility of Austria for the crimes of National Socialism. I also recall the speech by President Klestil in Jerusalem in 1994 and a number of other occasions that have promoted the debate about the role of Austria in the Third Reich, such as the establishment of a day of commemoration against violence and racism, as well as many events here in Parliament and also those two laws. It is also clear that this law could not correct all material damage or be remedied, but that it was a gesture of reconciliation, of a humanitarian gesture – also as an admission to the injustice that many victims of National Socialism were committed. For this, too – and this was especially important for Austrian companies – legal certainty has been gained about the fact that there will be no further claims, because otherwise some Austrian companies would have been threatened with new forced labour complaints. A chapter of Austrian post-war history has been completed, namely, after the years of reconstruction and the management of internal compensation, the necessary legal, political and moral commitments to the victims have been fulfilled. More than a million foreigners were forced by the National Socialist regime to work in the field of today's Austria, to slave and forced labour as an expression of a cruel disregard for human rights, which meant deportation, renunciation, enslavement and abuse, in many cases also death. Thus, at least those people who were directly affected by the atrocities of the Nazi regime encounter a minor satisfaction after Austria had already been freed from all future repair payments under the provisions of the Austrian State Treaty of 1955. In this light the amount of 6 billion schilling is fair and appropriate for years of suffering, for free work and severe physical and psychological damage. In this case, not only the material reparation, but also the moral-spiritual dimension is in the foreground, which is why some newspaper reports, according to which there appear to be not only considerable bureaucratic obstacles in disbursement, but also individual people, even in terms of their compensation, are all the more pitiful. It remains to be hoped that the most embarrassing chapter of Austrian history will not find another unworthy conclusion. It remains to be hoped that the National Fund, which was set up to work out a piece of Austrian history and the fate of persecuted people, will last beyond 2004 and will also be sufficiently taught to contribute to the memory of one of the darkest chapters of Austrian history. - Always stay on guard!

Point Report of the Committee on Education on the Government Template (8 of the Supplements): Federal Law on the External Legal Relations of the Oriental Orthodox Churches in Austria (Oriental Orthodox Church Law; OrientKG) (31 of the supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e18322	We're now on the 6th. Agenda item. The first speaker to speak was Mr Amon. His speaking time is gladly set to 3 minutes. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-03-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e18344	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We are now dealing with a government template that includes a federal law on external legal relations of the oriental Orthodox churches in Austria. It is a federal law which we had on the agenda at the last committee meeting of the Committee on Education, which was unanimously adopted by all four parliamentary parties in the Committee on Education. The oriental Orthodox churches in Austria have three traditional strands, namely the Syrian Orthodox Church, the Coptic Orthodox Church and the Armenian Orthodox Church. As part of this federal law, we deal with the Coptic Orthodox Church, which founded its original movement in Egypt. Austria's right of recognition is basically known to have three procedures relating to the recognition of religious communities. First of all, there is the possibility of the regulation by the Federal Minister on the basis of the Act of Recognition of 1874, which also relates to the status of a public body. The second possibility is recognition by a federal law of its own. – A number of churches in Austria are recognized by their own federal law, such as the Roman Catholic Church, the Protestant Church, the Greek-Oriental Church, Islam and the Israeli Church. The third possibility is recognition of the federal law on the legal personality of religious denominations. I would like to say briefly about the history of the recognition of the Orthodox churches: The Armenian Orthodox Church was already recognized in 1972 by a decree of the Federal Ministry or then the Federal Minister, and the Syrian Orthodox Church was recognized in 1988 by a corresponding regulation. It is now, as it were, a question of constitutional equal treatment also in connection with state church law, which provides for a formal parity that we also recognize the Coptic Orthodox Church through a federal law. I would also like to mention here that it was Cardinal Christoph Schönborn, who has personally been very committed to this recognition of the Coptic Church in Austria, which after the last census in 2001 counts about 3,000 members and which currently has legal status as a religious community through the Federal Law. In the sense of equal treatment, however, we want to adopt this federal law today. I think it is also an expression of a liberal rule of law and an open society, as we are dealing with religious communities in Austria, and it is certainly an essential contribution to international understanding, especially in these days. Thank you for your approval.

In view of the fact that only about 1,600 people have actually called this belief in Austria in the census and are familiar with it, it can be assumed that in fact only small or no costs for religious education will arise, because these people are distributed throughout Austria, even though there are already and will be more centres of conurbation, especially in the big cities. If this does not result in costs, then that is certainly a welcome fact for the federal government, because overall we are in the process of implementing serious savings measures for the education sector. That is why this has so far been an unsatisfying state, and we are pleased that today we can adopt this law in the sense of a free open society and, above all, in the sense of freedom of religion, as has already been said.

If a neighbor or neighbor can require an environmental impact assessment, an independent procedure is no longer guaranteed. In particular, agriculture, which is close to residential areas or nature reserves, is not only affected by the proposed innovation, but is at high risk. The smell of animals is not everybody's business, but it belongs to a functioning agriculture. As proposed by the Federal Government, the attitude and breeding of animals should be governed by a uniform animal welfare law, not by a novel to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act. ==References== We will discuss this in detail at the animal welfare survey on April, and I hope that we will come to a reasonable solution. I do not think that this innovation, as you propose, would affect only intensiveness, but of course also organic and small agriculture. It is also not to be seen that this innovation carries the impression of a “positive” and a “negative” agriculture outside. Let us discuss in the committees how we can all help our farmers to change, improve something, and do not rely on forced measures that are being carried out on the back of these! Direct investment support for environmental measures in agricultural installations would be much more helpful. The burden cannot and must not be one-sided in the agricultural sector, but our farmers need all our support to ensure that active agriculture is part of our lives in the future.
